# PR #19172: DRA: search other devices when a CEL request falls short

**Summary**: DRA: search other devices when a CEL request falls short

## Description

#### What type of PR is this?

/kind bug
/area dra

#### What this PR does / why we need it?

The CEL device check gives each request of a ResourceClaimTemplate the first devices that match and never revisits that choice. When an earlier request takes a device that a later request needs, the check reports a shortage, even when another choice would give every request its own matching device.

When a request falls short, the check now tries other device choices before it reports the shortage. For the same list of devices, it tries requests, alternatives and devices in the order of the kube-scheduler allocator, but tries each set of devices for a request only once. It drops a choice as soon as too few unused devices are left that could match. The search stops at the first CEL evaluation error, and the claim then keeps its shortage. It also stops after 1,000,000 device visits, not counting the first pass, and the check then lets the claim through: kube-scheduler still decides per node, and each retry would stop at the same limit.

The shortage is an internal error, so the workload controller retries the claim with backoff. Only the first request that falls short is reported now, with the errors that the check reaches; during the search they can come from later requests. As on main, a request that stops at an evaluation error keeps the devices it matched before the error; #19158 released them for a `firstAvailable` alternative. In the search, a request with `adminAccess`, `allocationMode: All` and a CEL selector holds every device in the cluster that it matches. That choice fails when nothing matches or when an earlier request holds one of the matches, as in the allocator. The first pass still holds only its first unused matching device, as main does.

Fixes #19161

#### Useful notes for your reviewer

`go test -race -count=1 ./pkg/dra/` passes. Of the 23 new `Test_GetResourceRequests` cases, 15 fail on main (f1314eb02). The three new integration specs pass here and fail on main. One of them makes the search reach a CEL error, then fixes only the ResourceSlice, and checks that the Workload then reserves quota without any change to it. Another one reaches the step limit and checks that the Workload reserves quota. The unit case that reaches the step limit takes about 0.01 s. The slowest new case lists 40,000 devices in one ResourceSlice, which only the fake client accepts, and takes about 0.5 s, or up to 3.5 s with `-race`.

Outside this PR, I compared the result (match, shortage or error) with the stable, incubating and experimental allocators of `k8s.io/dynamic-resource-allocation` v0.37.2. Each input sets three selectors to true, false or a missing-attribute error on each of three devices in one ResourceSlice. That is 19,683 inputs for each of two claims: three `exactly` requests, and a two-alternative `firstAvailable` request followed by an `exactly` request. The check agrees with all three allocators on every input; #19158 differs on 2,196 and 1,024 of them. Over 584 claims on one node with two `exactly` requests and an `adminAccess` request in `allocationMode: All`, the check now admits the 5 that main rejects and the allocators can place, and nothing else that main rejects.

With evaluation errors, the result still depends on device order, as in the allocator. Kueue walks the devices of the whole cluster in listing order, which can change between reconciles, as main already does for `exactly` requests. kube-scheduler walks one node's devices: its incubating and experimental allocators sort pools by driver and pool name and slices by name, and the stable allocator walks pools in map order. Without evaluation errors or an `adminAccess` request with `allocationMode: All`, the order changes nothing unless the search reaches the step limit. Like main, the check does not know the Pod's node, so it can count devices on different nodes for one claim; the search finds such sets in more listing orders than the greedy check.

The search covers the same requests as the check on main: an `exactly` request with a CEL selector, and a `firstAvailable` request with one on every alternative. It does not see the devices that other requests need, so it can pass a claim that main rejects and no node can place. For example: devices a, b and c on one node, and requests for a or b, only a, and two devices without a selector. #19258 tracks it.

This PR also updates three KEP lines from #18983 that the search makes stale: the check "does not backtrack" (L543-544), it "scans the devices once per alternative it tries" (L1017-1018), and it "stops at the first" `firstAvailable` alternative that matches enough devices (L2303). It also adds to `site/content/en/docs/tasks/run/dra.md` that Kueue admits a workload when the search gives up on its claim.

#### Release note

```release-note
DRA: Fixed a bug where a Workload whose ResourceClaimTemplate has several device requests with CEL selectors could stay pending with "insufficient matching devices", even when the cluster has enough matching devices for all the requests at once.
```

_Disclosure: I used an AI coding assistant while preparing this PR._

With prioritized-list support now enabled by default, some satisfiable DRA claims can be held pending indefinitely. This can happen when a claim also uses AdminAccess with AllocationMode All. It can also happen when a CEL evaluation error is reached on the first pass, because a later device fix may not requeue the workload. Several previously raised matching-accuracy issues also remain open. These problems block workloads rather than corrupt data, and disabling the gate restores the earlier behavior. They should still be resolved or explicitly accepted before this ships as Beta.
<!-- final_review_risk_end -->

**Explanation**

The title "DRA: search other devices when a CEL request falls short" matches the main change: the claim-validation logic in pkg/dra/claims.go now searches alternative device choices when a CEL-selector request cannot be satisfied on the first pass, and the PR objectives state this same goal. <details>
<summary><strong>Full details: Linked Issues check</strong></summary>
<dl>
<dd>
<br>

**Explanation**

The only directly linked issue is `#19161`. Its coding requirements and the evidence for each:

- Keep device choices provisional and backtrack across requests [`#19161`]: The pkg/dra/claims.go matcher tries requests, alternatives, and devices in allocator order while it assigns distinct devices. It drops a choice when too few unused matching devices remain. The search runs only after the first pass falls short.
- Apply to both Exactly and firstAvailable requests [`#19161`]: The claims.go summary states that firstAvailable alternatives enter the same CEL search. The search is skipped when a CEL evaluation error occurs or when a request uses allocationMode All. Neither condition applies to the issue's reproduction, which uses CEL-selected devices and no error or All mode.
- Preserve the first firstAvailable alternative that permits a complete assignment [`#19161`]: The in-order search returns the first complete assignment, so earlier alternatives take precedence. No named test case checks this preference directly. The conclusion rests on the search order in claims.go and on the PR description.
- Continue to report genuine shortages [`#19161`]: The Test_GetResourceRequests additions include an unsatisfiable case. The search stops at the step limit and keeps the shortage.
- Regression coverage through GetResourceRequestsForResourceClaimTemplates [`#19161`]: The claims_test.go changes add Exactly-then-Exactly and firstAvailable-then-Exactly conflict shapes through Test_GetResourceRequests. The author reports 15 of 20 new cases fail on the `#19158` base. An integration spec also covers an earlier CEL request that moves to another device.

Limits: The current head changed since the previous review. This assessment relies on the whole-PR summary and the stated test results. Tests were not run in this assessment. The first-alternative preference lacks a direct named test. The 1,000,000 visit limit and the CEL-error stop rule are implementation choices that the issue does not set and that do not contradict it.

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

</dd>
</dl>
</details>

- keps/2941-DRA/README.md: promotes KueueDRAIntegrationPrioritizedList to Beta.

The claims.go, claims_test.go, and pools.go changes relate to `#19161`. The stacked base does not remove the `#19158` changes from this PR's diff. The author's hold and rebase plan do not change the current diff.

**Resolution**

After `#19158` merges, rebase this PR onto the updated base. Confirm that the whole-PR diff contains only the bounded search commit and the changes it directly needs in pkg/dra/claims.go, pkg/dra/claims_test.go, and pkg/dra/pools.go. Remove the feature-gate, documentation, end-to-end, and `#19158` integration changes from this PR, or move them to a PR that targets their own issue. If a non-search change must remain, justify it in the PR description with a specific link to `#19161`.

### Comment by [@thc1006](https://github.com/thc1006) — 2026-11-20T06:19:38Z

Done in cf0563567. The step-limit case now uses a claim that fits only past the limit, a CEL error that the search reaches comes with the shortage so the Workload is still retried, and the search drops a choice once too few unused devices can match. The shortage counts, driver filtering, KEP MultiKueue row and two e2e comments are on #19158's code. I'll add the step limit to `dra.md` when I rebase.

### Comment by [@thc1006](https://github.com/thc1006) — 2026-11-20T07:50:55Z

Done in eede02f0e. A request that stops at a CEL evaluation error keeps the devices it matched, as on main, so the later request still reports its shortage. A shortage on every evaluation error would also retry a single failing request, which main doesn't. `fits` stops counting once enough devices may match and charges every device it visits; its callers check the limit right after it returns. The integration spec checks quota reservation, like the other "Should admit" specs there.

### Comment by [@thc1006](https://github.com/thc1006) — 2026-11-20T11:31:53Z

Done in f62d6e2f7: the shortage now says when the search reached its limit, the search checks the limit before each visit, and the limit's comment excludes the first pass. The check joins every ResourceSlice into one device list, so one 40,000-device slice takes the same path as 313 valid ones. Device order and node scope come from the check, which main already runs over the whole cluster in listing order for `exactly` requests; #19158 extends it to `firstAvailable`, and this PR changes neither.

### Comment by [@thc1006](https://github.com/thc1006) — 2026-11-20T14:05:26Z

Done in efd9b1a2b: a claim with an `adminAccess` request in `allocationMode: All` keeps its shortage and skips the search. Before, the search let through claims that main rejects and kube-scheduler cannot allocate, with an overlap or an empty match; new cases cover both. Reserving every match would not work, since the check runs across nodes before a node is picked. Without a shortage nothing changes, so a lone `All` request with no match still passes, as on main. The shared device case is #19179.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Functional Correctness** · **Heavy lift**
  
  **Keep driver filtering for source-backed requests in mixed claims.**
  
  Before this change, a claim with a capacity-backed `Exactly` request and a source-less `firstAvailable` request checked only the exact request’s configured driver. Now the prioritized alternative causes `ListAll`, and the matcher gives both requests every device. If a device from driver B comes first, an exact selector that reads driver A’s `profile` can return `Invalid` before reaching a matching driver A device. The DeviceClass need not have a driver selector of its own. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/95be404a101ba835b29afd62a678733f03eaa967/pkg/dra/claims.go))
  
  Keep a shared device list for distinctness, but restrict each source-backed alternative to its configured drivers. Leave source-less alternatives free to search all drivers.

  **Keep backoff when a CEL error is reached only during backtracking.**
  
  The case at Line 1263 in `pkg/dra/claims_test.go` used to return a retryable shortage: the first two requests took A and B, and the third could not use C. Backtracking now reaches the second request’s CEL error on C and returns only `Invalid`. After persisting the conditions, `markDRAInadmissible` returns nil for that result, so the workload loses its backoff retry.   **Skip alternatives already proven too small before spending the search budget.**
  
  Consider 22 devices. A `firstAvailable` request needs 21: its preferred alternative matches only the first 20, and its fallback matches all 22. A later `Exactly` request needs the first device. The first pass proves that the preferred alternative is too small, then takes devices 1–21 with the fallback. Backtracking starts over with the preferred alternative and exhausts the cap enumerating subsets of its 20 devices. It never tries the valid assignment: devices 2–22 for the fallback and device 1 for the exact request. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/95be404a101ba835b29afd62a678733f03eaa967/pkg/dra/claims.go))
  
  Use a complete per-alternative match count to prune branches that cannot fit. Do not prune from first-pass counts alone when earlier requests hid devices that backtracking can release.

  **Select the hostname label value from the Node object, not from the node name.**
  
  Lines 756-757 store the slice's `spec.nodeName`. Line 781 then uses that value as the `kubernetes.io/hostname` label selector. Kubernetes does not guarantee that the hostname label equals the node name. If the label differs, no node matches the selector. Both Pods stay Pending, and the allocation assertion times out.
  
  Read the Node object named by `nodeName` and use the value of its `kubernetes.io/hostname` label in `NodeSelector`. Fail with a clear message if that label is absent.

+// maxCELSearchSteps caps the devices that the search visits for one claim.
+// Past it, the claim keeps its shortage.
+const maxCELSearchSteps = 1_000_000
+
+// celDeviceMatcher finds distinct devices for the CEL requests of one claim.
+// Like the kube-scheduler allocator, it tries requests, alternatives and
+// devices in order and stops at the first CEL evaluation error, so the errors
+// it returns depend on the device order.
+type celDeviceMatcher struct {
+	cl         client.Client
+	devices    []dracel.Device
+	requests   []celDeviceRequest
+	basePath   *field.Path
+	classCache map[string]*resourcev1.DeviceClass
+	// alternatives[r][a] is alternative a of request r, set on first use.
+	alternatives [][]*celAlternativeMatcher
+	// used marks the devices that the current choice takes.
+	used []bool
+	// steps counts the devices that the search has visited.
+	steps int
+}
+
+func newCELDeviceMatcher(cl client.Client, devices []dracel.Device, requests []celDeviceRequest, basePath *field.Path) *celDeviceMatcher {
+	m := &celDeviceMatcher{
+		cl:           cl,
+		devices:      devices,
+		requests:     requests,
+		basePath:     basePath,
+		classCache:   make(map[string]*resourcev1.DeviceClass),
+		alternatives: make([][]*celAlternativeMatcher, len(requests)),
+		used:         make([]bool, len(devices)),
+	}
+	for r, cr := range requests {
+		m.alternatives[r] = make([]*celAlternativeMatcher, len(cr.alternatives))
+	}
+	return m
+}
+
+// celAlternativeMatcher evaluates the selectors of one alternative, once per device.
+type celAlternativeMatcher struct {
+	deviceClassName string
+	selectorsPath   *field.Path
+	classSelectors  []dracel.CompilationResult
+	selectors       []dracel.CompilationResult
+	// results holds the result for each device; zero means not evaluated yet.
+	results []celMatchResult
+}
+
+type celMatchResult uint8
+
+const (
+	celMatching celMatchResult = iota + 1
+	celNotMatching
+)
 
+// check returns the first shortage that takeFirstMatches finds, unless the
+// search finds distinct devices for every request: an earlier request can take
+// the only devices that a later request can use. The shortage stands when the
+// search runs out of steps or stops at a CEL evaluation error.
+func (m *celDeviceMatcher) check(ctx context.Context) field.ErrorList {
+	shortage, errs := m.takeFirstMatches(ctx)
+	if shortage == nil {
+		return errs
+	}
+	// After an error, the search would stop at the same error.
+	if errs == nil {
+		clear(m.used)
+		var found bool
+		if found, errs = m.search(ctx, 0); found {
+			return nil
+		}
+		if m.outOfSteps() {
+			ctrl.LoggerFrom(ctx).V(4).Info("Stopped searching for devices that match the CEL selectors", "field", shortage.Field)
+		}
+	}
+	// An evaluation error does not replace the shortage, so that the workload
+	// controller still retries the check.
+	return append(errs, shortage)
+}
+
+// takeFirstMatches gives each request in turn its first matching devices. It
+// returns the shortage of the first request that falls short, and the errors of
+// the requests before it.
+func (m *celDeviceMatcher) takeFirstMatches(ctx context.Context) (*field.Error, field.ErrorList) {
 	var allErrs field.ErrorList
+	for r := range m.requests {
+		shortage, errs := m.takeFirstMatch(ctx, r)
+		allErrs = append(allErrs, errs...)
+		if shortage != nil {
+			return shortage, allErrs
+		}
+	}
+	return nil, allErrs

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Stability & Availability** · **Quick win**
  
  **Keep the shortage for a request that stops at a CEL evaluation error.**
  
  When the first request stops at an evaluation error, the claim returns only the `Invalid` error. A later request can then take the device that the first request matched. `markDRAInadmissible` returns a backoff error only when the list contains an `InternalError`, so this claim is not requeued with backoff. The base code produced that `InternalError` for the later request because it kept the device marked. Once the driver fixes the failing device, the claim can be satisfied, but the workload may remain pending until another event reconciles it.
  
  Keep the first request's shortage in the result along with its evaluation error. The claim must then stay retryable through the existing `InternalError` path. Do not restore the device reservation made by the failed request.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Performance & Scalability** · **Quick win**
  
  **Enforce the search budget inside `fits`.**
  
  `fits` charges the full suffix before scanning it but does not stop if that charge exceeds the remaining budget. If the suffix cannot fit, `fits` then scans it again and may evaluate CEL without charging those visits. The caller checks the budget only after `fits` returns. This can surface a CEL error from a device the search should not have reached. Check and charge visits in both loops before inspecting each device. Preserve in-order evaluation while budget remains. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/cf0563567857493c4b2bb9772af32542b57905d9/pkg/dra/claims.go))

  <!-- coderabbit-ai-generated:v1 -->
  
  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Performance & Scalability** · **Quick win**
  
  **Stop counting once the branch has enough possible matches.**
  
  With 100,000 eligible devices, let the first nine requests each match a different device, A through I. Let request 10 match J or K and request 11 match only J. Greedy takes J for request 10. During replay, `fits` scans all 100,000 devices for each prefix request, even when the first unused device is enough. The tenth scan exhausts the budget before request 10 can try K. The same inventory and order can leave the workload pending on every retry. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/cf0563567857493c4b2bb9772af32542b57905d9/pkg/dra/claims.go))
  
  Return as soon as `possible` reaches `remaining`, and charge only the entries visited. Keep the full check when there are too few possible matches.

Review comments at @pkg/dra/claims_test.go:
- Line 1545: Reduce the device inventory in the early-stop regression around the
Devices fixture to fit within Kubernetes’ 128-device-per-slice limit, and
configure the existing budget so counting only the required devices reaches K
while scanning the full inventory exhausts it. Keep the proof at the unit-test
level and avoid relying on an invalid oversized ResourceSlice.

Review comments at @pkg/dra/claims.go:
- Line 645: Update the CEL matching path around newCELDeviceMatcher and
buildDeviceListFromSlices so a CEL error rejects a claim only when evaluation
follows the applicable allocator’s known pool order; when that order is
unavailable, do not treat an error caused by cluster-listing order as
conclusive. Add a regression case with opposing slice-name and pool-name order,
where the allocator can satisfy a preferred alternative from the matching pool.
- Line 645: Update the availability check around newCELDeviceMatcher and
buildDeviceListFromSlices so CEL errors from devices on ineligible nodes do not
reject the workload. Evaluate only candidates on nodes established as eligible;
when Kueue cannot establish node eligibility, defer the blocking decision to the
scheduler. Preserve CEL errors for devices the allocator would actually
consider.
- Around line 719-725: Update the shortage construction around m.outOfSteps() so
search exhaustion adds a distinct detail to the workload condition while
retaining the retryable InternalError. Keep genuine shortage diagnostics
unchanged when the search limit did not prevent a decision.
- Line 754: Clarify the documented scope of the visit limit in the
claim-selection logic around takeFirstMatch: state that it bounds only
backtracking work charged by pick and fits, not greedy device visits. Keep the
current search behavior unchanged unless the implementation deliberately counts
greedy visits too; if it does, account for how reaching the budget affects
satisfiable claims and shortages.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/neutral-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Trivial** · **Maintainability & Code Quality** · **Quick win**
  
  **Use a smaller inventory for the early-stop regression.**
  
  The fake client accepts all 40,000 devices in one `ResourceSlice`. Kubernetes v1.37.2 permits at most 128 ordinary devices per slice. A smaller configurable budget would let this test use a valid slice and still distinguish the two paths: counting only what is needed reaches K, but scanning the full inventory for each pick exhausts the budget. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes/api/v0.37.2/resource/v1/types.go))
  
  As per path instructions, “Choose the lowest test level that can prove the behavior”; this can remain a unit test. AI-assisted review.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Functional Correctness** · **Heavy lift**
  
  **Account for scheduler pool order before treating a CEL error as conclusive.**
  
  Even on one node, the order can differ. Suppose slice `a-error` belongs to `pool-z` and slice `z-match` belongs to `pool-a`, with the same driver in both pools. If the list returns `a-error` first, Kueue keeps that order and stops on its CEL error. The v0.37.2 incubating allocator visits `pool-a` first. It satisfies a count-one preferred alternative there and never evaluates `pool-z`. Kueue therefore blocks a claim that this allocator can satisfy. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/eede02f0eb2f3eb2402d1b751326760cbb915746/pkg/dra/pools.go))
  
  Use the applicable allocator’s order where it is known. Otherwise, do not make a CEL error from the cluster listing order a definitive rejection. Add a regression case with opposing slice and pool name order.
  
  AI-assisted review.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Functional Correctness** · **Heavy lift**
  
  **Do not reject a workload on a CEL error from an ineligible node.**
  
  Consider a Pod restricted to node A. The same driver publishes a matching device on A and a device without `profile` on node B. If B’s slice is listed first, the new `firstAvailable` check evaluates `profile == "fast"` on B and returns `Invalid`. The workload controller stops admission. The scheduler excludes B’s slice before it evaluates the selector and can allocate the device on A. Before this availability check covered `firstAvailable`, Kueue did not block that allocation. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/eede02f0eb2f3eb2402d1b751326760cbb915746/pkg/dra/pools.go))
  
  Evaluate against candidates on eligible nodes, or defer the blocking decision to the scheduler when Kueue cannot establish node eligibility. Preserve CEL errors for devices the allocator would actually consider.
  
  AI-assisted review.

@@ -633,56 +642,307 @@ func validateCELSelectorsAgainstDevices(
 		}
 	}
 
-	clusterDevices := buildDeviceListFromSlices(ctx, collectedSlices)
-	classCache := make(map[string]*resourcev1.DeviceClass)
-	matchedDevices := sets.New[int]()
+	return newCELDeviceMatcher(cl, buildDeviceListFromSlices(ctx, collectedSlices), celReqs, basePath).check(ctx)
+}
+
+// maxCELSearchSteps caps the devices that the search visits for one claim.
+// Past it, the claim keeps its shortage.
+const maxCELSearchSteps = 1_000_000
+
+// celDeviceMatcher finds distinct devices for the CEL requests of one claim.
+// Like the kube-scheduler allocator, it tries requests, alternatives and
+// devices in order and stops at the first CEL evaluation error, so the errors
+// it returns depend on the device order.
+type celDeviceMatcher struct {
+	cl         client.Client
+	devices    []dracel.Device
+	requests   []celDeviceRequest
+	basePath   *field.Path
+	classCache map[string]*resourcev1.DeviceClass
+	// alternatives[r][a] is alternative a of request r, set on first use.
+	alternatives [][]*celAlternativeMatcher
+	// used marks the devices that the current choice takes.
+	used []bool
+	// steps counts the devices that the search has visited.
+	steps int
+}
+
+func newCELDeviceMatcher(cl client.Client, devices []dracel.Device, requests []celDeviceRequest, basePath *field.Path) *celDeviceMatcher {
+	m := &celDeviceMatcher{
+		cl:           cl,
+		devices:      devices,
+		requests:     requests,
+		basePath:     basePath,
+		classCache:   make(map[string]*resourcev1.DeviceClass),
+		alternatives: make([][]*celAlternativeMatcher, len(requests)),
+		used:         make([]bool, len(devices)),
+	}
+	for r, cr := range requests {
+		m.alternatives[r] = make([]*celAlternativeMatcher, len(cr.alternatives))
+	}
+	return m
+}
+
+// celAlternativeMatcher evaluates the selectors of one alternative, once per device.
+type celAlternativeMatcher struct {
+	deviceClassName string
+	selectorsPath   *field.Path
+	classSelectors  []dracel.CompilationResult
+	selectors       []dracel.CompilationResult
+	// results holds the result for each device; zero means not evaluated yet.
+	results []celMatchResult
+}
+
+type celMatchResult uint8
 
+const (
+	celMatching celMatchResult = iota + 1
+	celNotMatching
+)
+
+// check returns the first shortage that takeFirstMatches finds, unless the
+// search finds distinct devices for every request: an earlier request can take
+// the only devices that a later request can use. The shortage stands when the
+// search runs out of steps or stops at a CEL evaluation error.
+func (m *celDeviceMatcher) check(ctx context.Context) field.ErrorList {
+	shortage, errs := m.takeFirstMatches(ctx)
+	if shortage == nil {
+		return errs
+	}
+	// After an error, the search would stop at the same error.
+	if errs == nil {
+		clear(m.used)
+		var found bool
+		if found, errs = m.search(ctx, 0); found {
+			return nil
+		}
+		if m.outOfSteps() {
+			ctrl.LoggerFrom(ctx).V(4).Info("Stopped searching for devices that match the CEL selectors", "field", shortage.Field)
+		}
+	}
+	// An evaluation error does not replace the shortage, so that the workload
+	// controller still retries the check.
+	return append(errs, shortage)
```

  **Expose search exhaustion in the workload condition.**
  
  The satisfiable claim in the search-limit test returns the same shortage as a claim with no valid assignment. The only distinction is a `V(4)` log.   **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Performance & Scalability** · **Quick win**
  
  **State the scope of the 1,000,000 visit limit, or bound the greedy pass as well.**
  
  The step counter increases only in `pick` and `fits`. `takeFirstMatch` scans every device for each alternative and does not charge those visits. With eight alternatives and 125,001 devices that match none of them, the greedy pass makes 1,000,008 visits before the limit applies. The limit therefore does not cap the total visits per claim.
  
  The cost stays bounded. Work grows with alternatives times devices, and each alternative–device pair evaluates CEL at most once because the result is cached. The problem is that the documented limit does not describe the actual work.
  
  Either document that the limit covers only the backtracking search, or count greedy visits toward the budget. Counting them can stop the search early for a satisfiable claim and retain a shortage, so choose that policy deliberately.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Functional Correctness** · **Heavy lift**
  
  **Reserve every matching device for `AllocationMode: All`.**
  
  On one node, let the search move the first two requests to B and A. If an AdminAccess `All` request matches C and D, `holdFirst` reserves only C. A later request can then take D, and Kueue discards the earlier shortage. The allocator requires both C and D for the `All` request, so the workload can hold quota without being schedulable. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes/dynamic-resource-allocation/v0.37.2/structured/internal/stable/allocator_stable.go))
  
  If this branch remains supported, compute the full matching set before advancing. Reject a branch if an earlier request holds any required device. Use the same rule in the first pass, and release the full provisional set when the branch returns.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Functional Correctness** · **Quick win**
  
  **Do not advance when an `All` request matches no devices.**
  
  With devices A and B, the search can move the first request to B and give A to the second. If the final AdminAccess `All` request matches neither device, this call still reaches the success base case. Kueue then discards the shortage, although the allocator requires at least one match for `All`. Reserving every match would not fix the empty-set case. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes/dynamic-resource-allocation/v0.37.2/structured/internal/stable/allocator_stable.go))
  
  Return an unsuccessful branch for an empty set. In the first pass, report a retryable shortage instead of accepting zero picks against count zero.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Functional Correctness** · **Heavy lift**
  
  **Do not exclude a device that can satisfy another capacity share.**
  
  Consider one device with `AllowMultipleAllocations=true` and `8Gi` of capacity. Two `firstAvailable` requests in the same claim each ask for one `4Gi` share. The first request marks the device as used. The second request skips it in both the first pass and the search. The resulting shortage keeps the workload pending even though the v0.37.2 allocator can assign both shares. Kueue documents whole-device quota charging for this configuration; that charge does not make the physical device exclusive. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes/dynamic-resource-allocation/v0.37.2/structured/internal/incubating/allocator_incubating.go))
  
  Track remaining capacity for devices that allow multiple allocations, or defer the shortage decision when the matcher cannot rule out sharing. Apply the same rule in the first pass and `fits`. Keep exclusive devices distinct.

  **Assert that the shortage identifies search exhaustion.**
  
  This fixture now distinguishes capped search from uncapped search. It still passes if `check` stops at the cap but omits the exhaustion detail. The table runner ignores `Detail`, so an ordinary shortage at the same path satisfies the assertion. +
+// maxCELSearchSteps caps the device visits of the search after the first pass.
+const maxCELSearchSteps = 1_000_000
+
+// celDeviceMatcher finds distinct devices for the CEL requests of one claim. Like
+// the kube-scheduler allocator, its search tries requests, alternatives and
+// devices in order and stops at the first CEL evaluation error.
+type celDeviceMatcher struct {
+	cl         client.Client
+	devices    []dracel.Device
+	requests   []celDeviceRequest
+	basePath   *field.Path
+	classCache map[string]*resourcev1.DeviceClass
+	// alternatives[r][a] matches requests[r].alternatives[a]; nil until first use.
+	alternatives [][]*celAlternativeMatcher
+	// used[d] reports whether a request holds devices[d].
+	used []bool
+	// steps counts device visits against maxCELSearchSteps.
+	steps int
+}
+
+func newCELDeviceMatcher(cl client.Client, devices []dracel.Device, requests []celDeviceRequest, basePath *field.Path) *celDeviceMatcher {
+	m := &celDeviceMatcher{
+		cl:           cl,
+		devices:      devices,
+		requests:     requests,
+		basePath:     basePath,
+		classCache:   make(map[string]*resourcev1.DeviceClass),
+		alternatives: make([][]*celAlternativeMatcher, len(requests)),
+		used:         make([]bool, len(devices)),
+	}
+	for r, cr := range requests {
+		m.alternatives[r] = make([]*celAlternativeMatcher, len(cr.alternatives))
+	}
+	return m
+}
+
+// celAlternativeMatcher evaluates the selectors of one alternative on each device.
+type celAlternativeMatcher struct {
+	deviceClassName string
+	selectorsPath   *field.Path
+	classSelectors  []dracel.CompilationResult
+	selectors       []dracel.CompilationResult
+	// results[d] caches whether devices[d] matches; errors are not cached.
+	results []celMatchResult
+}
+
+type celMatchResult uint8
+
+const (
+	celNotEvaluated celMatchResult = iota
+	celMatching
+	celNotMatching
+)
 
+// check returns the errors that the first pass or the search reaches, and the
+// first shortage that takeFirstMatches finds, which the workload controller
+// retries. It returns nil when the search finds distinct devices for every request.
+func (m *celDeviceMatcher) check(ctx context.Context) field.ErrorList {
+	shortage, errs := m.takeFirstMatches(ctx)
+	if shortage == nil {
+		return errs

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Stability & Availability** · **Heavy lift**
  
  **A ResourceSlice repair does not re-admit a DRA workload blocked by a CEL error.**
  
  When the only `firstAvailable` request of a DRA workload returns a CEL evaluation error on the first pass, the workload is marked inadmissible and removed from the queue. The controller returns no backoff error for this non-internal result. A ResourceSlice update for a configured driver calls only `NotifyRetryInadmissible` for ClusterQueue inadmissible sets, and it does not reach the removed DRA workload. The workload can then remain pending until an unrelated event, such as a Workload or LocalQueue change, a DeviceClass event, or a generation change, enqueues it.
  
  The DRA path needs a required trigger. A ResourceSlice change that can resolve a pending DRA workload's CEL error should re-enqueue that workload, or the requeue should otherwise occur without an unrelated change.

  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Functional Correctness** · **Heavy lift**
  
  **Do not reject a satisfiable prioritized list because the claim also uses `All`.**
  
  On one node, let a count-one `firstAvailable` request match A or B, a later `Exactly` request match only A, and an AdminAccess `All` request match only C. The first pass gives A to `firstAvailable`. The `Exactly` request then falls short, and the `All` guard prevents search even though the first pass never reached that request. Before `firstAvailable` entered this check, it did not block quota for this claim. Now every retry with the same inventory returns the same shortage. The scheduler can assign B, A, and C. ([raw.githubusercontent.com](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes/dynamic-resource-allocation/v0.37.2/structured/internal/stable/allocator_stable.go))
  
  Support the full `All` match set during search, or defer the shortage when the precheck cannot rule out a valid assignment. Do not remove the guard while still modeling `All` as a single device.

  This case still passes with the pruning in pick removed: the search reaches the step limit and the limit now lets the claim through, so could it expect a shortage instead?

  Done in cbb20866b. With one spare device, "spare" reaches 18 only by taking dev-first, so the case now expects the shortage of only-first. The 40,000-device case had the same gap, so it now ends with a request whose broken DeviceClass selector only a search past only-j reaches. Removing the pruning in `pick` now fails the first case, and making `fits` count every device fails the second.
